<apps_instructions>
## Apps (Connectors)
Apps (Connectors) can be explicitly triggered in user messages in the format `[$app-name](app://{connector_id})`. Apps can also be implicitly triggered as long as the context suggests usage of available apps.
An app is equivalent to a set of MCP tools within the `codex_apps` MCP.
An installed app's MCP tools are either provided to you already, or can be lazy-loaded through the `tool_search` tool. If `tool_search` is available, the apps that are searchable by `tools_search` will be listed by it.
Do not additionally call list_mcp_resources or list_mcp_resource_templates for apps.
</apps_instructions>

<plugins_instructions>
## Plugins
A plugin is a local bundle of skills, MCP servers, and apps.
### How to use plugins
- Skill naming: If a plugin contributes skills, those skill entries are prefixed with `plugin_name:` in the Skills list.
- MCP naming: Plugin-provided MCP tools keep standard MCP identifiers such as `mcp__server__tool`; use tool provenance to tell which plugin they come from.
- Trigger rules: If the user explicitly names a plugin, prefer capabilities associated with that plugin for that turn.
- Relationship to capabilities: Plugins are not invoked directly. Use their underlying skills, MCP tools, and app tools to help solve the task.
- Relevance: Determine what a plugin can help with from explicit user mention or from the plugin-associated skills, MCP tools, and apps exposed elsewhere in this turn.
- Missing/blocked: If the user requests a plugin that does not have relevant callable capabilities for the task, say so briefly and continue with the best fallback.
</plugins_instructions>

## developer

You are `/root`, the primary agent in a team of agents collaborating to fulfill the user's goals.

At the start of your turn, you are the active agent.
You can spawn sub-agents to handle subtasks, and those sub-agents can spawn their own sub-agents.
All agents in the team, including the agents that you can assign tasks to, are equally intelligent and capable, and have access to the same set of tools.

You can use `spawn_agent` to create a new agent, `followup_task` to give an existing agent a new task and trigger a turn, and `send_message` to pass a message to a running agent without triggering a turn.
Child agents can also spawn their own sub-agents.
You can decide how much context you want to propagate to your sub-agents with the `fork_turns` parameter.

You will receive messages in the analysis channel in the form:
```
Message Type: MESSAGE | FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: <recipient>
Sender: <author>
Payload:
<payload text>
```
They may be addressed as to=/root

Note that collaboration tools cannot be called from inside `functions.exec`. Call `spawn_agent`, `send_message`, `followup_task`, `wait_agent`, `interrupt_agent`, and `list_agents` only as direct tool calls using the recipient shown in their tool definitions, such as `to=functions.collaboration.spawn_agent`, since they are intentionally absent from the `functions.exec` `tools.*` namespace. Available tools in `functions.exec` are explicitly described with a `tools` namespace in the developer message.

All agents share the same directory. In detail:
- All agents have access to the same container and filesystem as you.
- All agents use the same current working directory.
- As a result, edits made by one agent are immediately visible to all other agents.

When calling `wait_agent`, prefer longer waits (minutes) to avoid busy polling.

There are 4 available concurrency slots, meaning that up to 4 agents can be active at once, including you.

Full-history forks (`fork_turns` omitted or `"all"`) inherit the parent model and reasoning effort and do not accept overrides. Only set `model` or `reasoning_effort` when explicitly requested by the user, applicable `AGENTS.md` instructions, or skill instructions; when doing so, set `fork_turns` to `"none"` or a positive integer string.

## developer

<multi_agent_mode>Any earlier instruction enabling proactive multi-agent delegation no longer applies. Do not spawn sub-agents unless the user or applicable AGENTS.md/skill instructions explicitly ask for sub-agents, delegation, or parallel agent work.</multi_agent_mode>

# AGENTS.md instructions for C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\lsatu

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# Global rules — Joshua

These apply on **every** project on this machine, in every session. Project-level
`AGENTS.md` files add to this; they do not replace it.

Keep this file short. It reloads on every message.

---

## Who

Joshua Fisseha — `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`. Builds **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study) and
the Aspiring Attorneys brand. Teaches the LSAT: ~25 one-to-one sessions a week plus
consultations. Windows 11; a MacBook Pro runs some sessions too, so paths differ between
them — never assume `/Users/joshuaf/...` resolves here.

## Where things live

- **AI OS / second brain:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` — the Obsidian vault. `context/`,
  `wiki/`, `students/`, `corpus/`, `decisions/`, `brainstorms/`, `skills/`, `projects/`.
  Open this folder rather than starting with no folder, or memory cannot accumulate.
- **Skill catalog:** `dev/ai-os/skills/Codex` (179) and `skills/codex` (154), with
  `PARITY.md` declaring deliberate single-runtime skills and `INDEX.md` cataloging them.
- **LSAT U app:** repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` — React + Vite + TS + Tailwind +
  shadcn, Supabase backend, hosted on Lovable.
- **GitHub:** account `vnjkoxpl-42905` (auto-generated name, correct account).

## How to work with him

- **Do the whole task.** Deliver the finished thing, then say plainly what you skipped
  and why. Don't hand back a plan when the work was asked for.
- **Look before asking.** If the answer is on disk or reachable through a connected tool,
  go get it. He has Firecrawl, Google Workspace (`gws`), and `gh` — use them.
- **Say what's actually true.** Distinguish verified from assumed, every time. If a step
  failed or was skipped, lead with that. A confident wrong answer costs more than "I
  don't know yet."
- **Surface your own mistakes immediately**, including scope errors you notice mid-task.
- Absolute dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), never "yesterday" or "last week".
- Don't re-ask what he already answered. Don't pad with options he didn't request.

- **Dictation fixes.** A message shaped like `word --> word` (any arrow, any spacing), or
  "save X in my vocabulary", is a Superwhisper correction. Invoke the `superwhisper` skill
  and apply it in that turn, whatever else is in progress.

## Writing as Joshua

Anything published under his name — course copy, lessons, emails, posts.

Plain, direct, confident. Short sentences. Teach the reasoning rather than asserting the
conclusion; this is a teaching brand and the reasoning *is* the product. Concrete beats
abstract. Never condescend to the reader.

**Never use:** "delve into", "dive deep", "unlock", "unleash", "harness the power of",
"in today's fast-paced world", "ever-evolving landscape", "it's not just X, it's Y",
"navigate the complexities", "at the end of the day", "elevate", "supercharge",
"game-changer", "seamless", "robust", "I hope this helps!", "Great question!",
"Certainly!". No em-dash pile-ups. No paragraph opening with "Moreover," /
"Furthermore," / "Additionally,". No emoji in professional writing unless asked.

Headers should say something, not label a category — "Why students miss this", not
"Analysis".

## Safety

- **Draft by default; send only when he says "send".** Email, messages, posts are drafted
  and shown to him. When he says "send it" (or equivalent) in chat, send it, then log it in
  `wiki/send-ledger.md`. Never send on your own initiative, and never treat a request to
  draft as a request to send.
- **Never delete** files, emails, or records without explicit confirmation.
- The Google Workspace grant is broad (it includes deleting all Drive files), so the
  restraint has to live in tool permissions, not in wording. A prompt is not a permission
  layer.
- Secrets stay in `.env` and the OS keyring. Never paste a key into a note, a commit, or
  a chat.

## Tools already connected

- **Firecrawl** — CLI + 28 skills. Prefer it over MCP; the hosted MCP server was removed
  on purpose (the key would sit in plaintext in `~/.Codex.json`).
- **Google Workspace** — `gws` CLI, GCP project `ai-os-joshua`. Gmail, Calendar, Drive,
  Docs, Sheets, Slides, Tasks, People.
- **GitHub** — `gh` CLI, authenticated.

## Cost discipline

`AGENTS.md` reloads on every message, so keep this file lean. Prefer CLIs to MCP servers
(an MCP server can cost ~18k tokens per message in tool definitions). `/clear` between
unrelated tasks. Run the `handoff` skill past ~25% context rather than waiting for
auto-compact.

---

point of the file.*

--- project-doc ---

# AGENTS.md

This file is the Codex-facing adapter for this repository. It summarizes the Claude-oriented project guidance from `CLAUDE.md` in a smaller format that is fast for Codex to scan. `CLAUDE.md` remains the fuller source of project knowledge.

## Fast Locator

- Vault root (the `~/ai-os` directory IS the Obsidian vault): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os`
- Code repo root (this project): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/projects/lsatu`
- Live vault knowledge: `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/wiki/` (hubs, voice, business), `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/students/`, `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/corpus/`
- Pre-2026-07-16 LSATU product knowledge (archived, read-only): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/archive/wiki-pre-rebuild-2026-07-16/domains/lsat-u/`

If an older note says `~/Documents/Obsidian Vault/...` or `~/Documents/Claude/02_PROJECTS/LSATU`, treat it as stale — the vault was consolidated into `~/ai-os`. Use the absolute paths above.

## Project Map

- `src/App.tsx`: route registration and top-level app wiring.
- `src/pages/`: route-level screens, including the Foyer and admin/teacher surfaces.
- `src/components/`: shared UI, including `foyer/`, `inbox/`, and shadcn/ui primitives.
- `src/hooks/`: auth, permissions, and shared data hooks.
- `src/lib/`: drill, WAJ, gamification, and other product logic.
- `src/integrations/supabase/`: generated Supabase client and types. Do not hand-edit generated types.
- `supabase/functions/`: Edge Functions.
- `supabase/migrations/`: database schema migrations.

## Stack And Commands

- Stack: React 18, Vite 5, TypeScript, Tailwind, shadcn/ui, Supabase, TanStack Query.
- Package manager: `npm`. `package-lock.json` is canonical. Ignore stray Bun lockfiles.
- Dev server: `npm run dev` on port `8080`.
- Build: `npm run build`
- Lint: `npm run lint`
- Test: `npm run test`
- Watch tests: `npm run test:watch`
- Typecheck: `npm run typecheck`. Required before a push — `vite build` strips types without checking them, so a type error would ship. `.claude/safe-push.sh` gates on it.
- E2E: `npm run test:e2e` (Playwright, `e2e/smoke.spec.ts`).

## Working Rules

- Keep changes small and aligned with existing patterns.
- Use TypeScript React function components and the `@/` path alias.
- Preserve Lovable-specific plumbing, including the `lovable-tagger` Vite plugin, the PWA setup, and the service-worker guard in `src/main.tsx`.
- Do not change app behavior just to satisfy tooling setup.
- Do not hardcode secrets or service-role credentials.
- Treat Supabase-generated files as generated artifacts, not hand-edited source.
- TypeScript strictness is intentionally loose. Do not tighten it without explicit approval.
- Keep this adapter aligned with `CLAUDE.md` without duplicating the full project guide. Put durable detail in `CLAUDE.md`; keep shared reference material in `docs/`, `references/`, or `templates/`.

## Product Constraints

- The Foyer is the primary navigation surface. The `GooeyDock` quick-launch row belongs only on `/foyer`.
- Inbox is a real 1:1 student-admin messaging system with PDF attachments.
- Admin surfaces must stay responsive on desktop and mobile.
- Prefer truthful fallbacks over fake buttons, fake analytics, or placeholder behavior.
- Onboarding asks for name, not username. Sign-in uses email. The instructor display name is `Joshua`.

## Smart Drill Note

On `/drill`, adaptive Smart Drill can show a `currentQuestion` that is not the same as `session.questionQueue[session.currentIndex]`. Any per-question UI state must key off `currentQuestion.qid` unless the code path is explicitly limited to queued drills.

## Verification (must not skip)

- Any change to `src/components/` or `src/pages/` is not "done" until it has been viewed running in the app (dev server + a browser tool), with a screenshot or DOM check as evidence. Never report a UI change complete on the strength of the diff alone. This is the single biggest recurring failure across past sessions — a hard gate, not a suggestion.
- **Responsive fixes carry a wider gate: verify across the full emulate matrix BEFORE pushing, not after Joshua reports it.** Narrow, mid, and wide viewports plus the sidecar-docked case, every time. A pushed "fix" that does not hold costs a second full session; this has already happened once.

## Workflow Expectations

- Run `npm run build` before wrapping up significant code or documentation work.
- If tests are relevant to the change, run them too.
- After completing a change, stage the intended files, commit with a concise message, and push to `origin main` unless the user says otherwise.
- **Push via `.claude/safe-push.sh`, not raw `git push`.** Lovable auto-commits to `main` mid-session, so a raw push can be rejected or race a concurrent commit. The script fetches, merges `origin/main` if it diverged (never rebases, never force-pushes), then pushes. It also gates on `npm run typecheck`.
- Existing Codex repo hooks live in `.codex/hooks.json`; preserve them unless the task explicitly requires a hook change.

## Session Protocol (second brain)

Product context, status, and decisions live in the Obsidian vault at `~/ai-os/`, not this repo. Two repo-local skills in `.agents/skills/` run the session ritual; the canonical protocol is in the vault `CLAUDE.md`.

The vault was rebuilt 2026-07-16 (paths relative to `~/ai-os`). Live: teaching voice `wiki/teaching-voice.md` (route ALL student-facing copy through it), the business `wiki/entities/aspiring-attorneys.md`, a student's record `students/<name>/` (see `students/README.md`), teaching knowledge `corpus/index.md`, and the `wiki/{tasks,hot,dashboard,index,log}.md` hubs named below. Archived read-only reference (pre-rebuild product knowledge, may be stale): `archive/wiki-pre-rebuild-2026-07-16/domains/lsat-u/` — product context `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-product.md`, design system `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-design.md`, quality bar `…/synthesis/bootcamp-quality-bar.md`, domain index `…/index.md`.

- `pickup` — start of session. Read `wiki/tasks.md` (the Now / Next / Done ledger) first, then `wiki/hot.md`, `wiki/dashboard.md`, skim `wiki/index.md`, read the latest `wiki/log.md` entry. Emit a 6-8 line briefing (where we left off, Now, Next, open threads, hot, suggested next) and wait. Do not deep-read other pages until pointed at a task.
- `wrapup` — end of session. Propose which `## Now` tasks finished and confirm before writing; a wrong "Done" is the failure to avoid. Then strike finished items through in `wiki/tasks.md` with a date, refresh `wiki/hot.md`, append a dated `wiki/log.md` entry (newest first, distinct label for same-day sessions), update the task anchor.

`wiki/tasks.md` is the single source of truth for what is open vs done. Finished work is struck through so a new session never re-runs it. `raw/` in the vault is immutable.

## Codex Adapter Layout

- `.codex/config.toml`: minimal shared Codex project config. Keep secrets and machine-specific overrides out of this file.
- `.codex/agents/`: Codex agent definitions converted from `.claude/agents/*.md` when Claude agents exist. Use `.toml` files with converted instructions in `developer_instructions`.
- `.agents/skills/`: repository-local Codex skills copied from `.claude/skills/` when Claude skills exist and Codex should use them. Keep each `SKILL.md` with its supporting files.
- `.codex/hooks.json`: existing repository hooks. Preserve unless the task explicitly concerns hooks.

This checkout currently has Claude settings and hooks under `.claude/`, but no `.claude/skills/` or `.claude/agents/` directory to convert.

## Known Gaps

- Vitest specs exist (80 `src/**/*.test.ts(x)` files as of 2026-07-16); run with `npm run test`. Playwright is wired with one e2e spec (`e2e/smoke.spec.ts`), run via `npm run test:e2e`.
- No Prettier config and no CI workflows.
- `README.md` is still minimal.

## Agent skills

### Issue tracker

### Triage labels

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 1.6 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '---\r\nname: review\r\ndescription: Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes - Standards (does the code follow this repo\'s documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/PRD asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to "review since X".\r\n---\r\n\r\nTwo-axis review of the diff between `HEAD` and a fixed point the user supplies:\r\n\r\n- **Standards** - does the code conform to this repo\'s documented coding standards?\r\n- **Spec** - does the code faithfully implement the originating issue / PRD / spec?\r\n\r\nBoth axes run as **parallel sub-agents** so they don\'t pollute each other\'s context, then this skill aggregates their findings.\r\n\r\nThe issue tracker should have been provided to you - run `/setup-matt-pocock-skills` if `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md` is missing.\r\n\r\n## Process\r\n\r\n### 1. Pin the fixed point\r\n\r\nWhatever the user said is the fixed point - a commit SHA, branch name, tag, `main`, `HEAD~5`, etc. If they didn\'t specify one, ask for it.\r\n\r\nCapture the diff command once: `git diff <fixed-point>...HEAD` (three-dot, so the comparison is against the merge-base). Also note the list of commits via `git log <fixed-point>..HEAD --oneline`.\r\n\r\nBefore going further, confirm the fixed point resolves (`git rev-parse <fixed-point>`) and the diff is non-empty. A bad ref or empty diff should fail here - not inside two parallel sub-agents.\r\n\r\n### 2. Identify the spec source\r\n\r\nLook for the originating spec, in this order:\r\n\r\n1. Issue references in the commit messages (`#123`, `Closes #45`, GitLab `!67`, etc.) - fetch via the workflow in `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.\r\n2. A path the user passed as an argument.\r\n3. A PRD/spec file under `docs/`, `specs/`, or `.scratch/` matching the branch name or feature.\r\n4. If nothing is found, ask the user where the spec is. If they say there isn\'t one, the **Spec** sub-agent will skip and report "no spec available".\r\n\r\n### 3. Identify the standards sources\r\n\r\nAnything in the repo that documents how code should be written, such as `CODING_STANDARDS.md` or `CONTRIBUTING.md`.\r\n\r\n### 4. Spawn both sub-agents in parallel\r\n\r\nSend a single message with two `Agent` tool calls. Use the `general-purpose` subagent for both.\r\n\r\n**Standards sub-agent prompt** - include:\r\n\r\n- The full diff command and commit list.\r\n- The list of standards-source files you found in step 3.\r\n- The brief: "Report - per file/hunk where relevant - every place the diff violates a documented standard. Cite the standard (file + the rule). Distinguish hard violations from judgement calls. Skip anything tooling enforces. Under 400 words."\r\n\r\n**Spec sub-agent prompt** - include:\r\n\r\n- The diff command and commit list.\r\n- The path or fetched contents of the spec.\r\n- The brief: "Report: (a) requirements the spec asked for that are missing or partial; (b) behaviour in the diff that wasn\'t asked for (scope creep); (c) requirements that look implemented but where the implementation looks wrong. Quote the spec line for each finding. Under 400 words."\r\n\r\nIf the spec is missing, skip the Spec sub-agent and note this in the final report.\r\n\r\n### 5. Aggregate\r\n\r\nPresent the two reports under `## Standards` and `## Spec` headings, verbatim or lightly cleaned. Do **not** merge or rerank findings - the two axes are deliberately separate (see _Why two axes_).\r\n\r\nEnd with a one-line summary: total findings per axis, and the worst issue _within each axis_ (if any). Don\'t pick a single winner across axes - that\'s the reranking the separation exists to prevent.\r\n\r\n## Why two axes\r\n\r\nA change can pass one axis and fail the other:\r\n\r\n- Code that follows every standard but implements the wrong thing \x1a **Standards pass, Spec fail.**\r\n- Code that does exactly what the issue asked but breaks the project\'s conventions \x1a **Spec pass, Standards fail.**\r\n\r\nReporting them separately stops one axis from masking the other.\r\n\r\n'}]

So Reset on an attempted question stores\r\n  183:   `{ pick: \'\' }` and can never fall back to the recorded answer.\r\n  184: - One accessor, `effectiveSelection(qid)`:\r\n  185:   `practiceByQid.get(qid)?.pick ?? selectedAnswer`. Every consumer reads\r\n  186:   it: `OfficialQuestionPane.selectedAnswer`, Reset\'s disabled state,\r\n  187:   `handleEliminateAnswer` (crossing out the effective pick clears it\r\n  188:   through the same writer), and `commitPendingSelection`.\r\n  189: - One writer, `setEffectiveSelection(qid, pick)`: writes `practiceByQid`\r\n  190:   when `session.attempts.has(qid)`, else `selectedAnswer`. Never touches\r\n  191:   `session.attempts` in these modes.\r\n  192: - `commitPendingSelection` stays\r\n  193:   `if (isAdaptiveMode || !selectedAnswer || currentAttempted || currentUnscored) return;`\r\n  194:   so a practice pick is never persisted: no `persistAttempt`, no\r\n  195:   `session.attempts` write, no journal, adaptive stats or resume rows.\r\n  196:   Student work is never modified after the fact. With Answer on, the\r\n  197:   colour follows the effective pick (a practice pick of the correct\r\n  198:   choice shows green; of a wrong one, red), which is what LSAT Lab does.\r\n  199: - The adaptive existing-attempt branch (`:1985-1995`) stops restoring\r\n  200:   `selectedAnswer` from the recorded attempt when a practice entry exists.\r\n  201: - `practiceByQid` is in-memory only, dropped on refresh; a practice pick is\r\n  202:   not student work.\r\n  203: \r\n  204: ### 5. Reset Response\r\n  205: \r\n  206: - `OfficialQuestionPane` gets `onResetResponse?: () => void` and renders a\r\n  207:   right-aligned ghost button "Reset Response" under the `<ul>`, `disabled`\r\n  208:   when `effectiveSelection(qid)` is empty. Rendered only when the prop is\r\n  209:   present (i.e. `reviewControlsAvailable`).\r\n  210: - Handler: `setEffectiveSelection(qid, \'\')`. Touches nothing else: not the\r\n  211:   timer, `session.attempts`, `showAnswerRevealed`, `explanationOpenByQid`,\r\n  212:   drawer, journal, or resume.\r\n  213: \r\n  214: ### 6. Persistence is honest and single-flight\r\n  215: \r\n  216: - `handleSubmitNonAdaptive` (`:1744-1785`) currently toasts on a failed\r\n  217:   save and still writes `session.attempts` and adaptive stats. Change: on\r\n  218:   `!saved`, toast and `return null` **before** any state write.\r\n  219: - **Every caller honours that null.** `commitPendingSelection` returns the\r\n  220:   result; `officialNext`, `officialPrev`, `handleJumpTo`, the pill\r\n  221:   pre-commit in step 2, and `handleFinishSection` (`:3868-3884`) stop on\r\n  222:   null: no navigation, no completion, the question stays with its pick and\r\n  223:   a toast. Test each caller with a failing `persistAttempt` mock.\r\n  224: - The single-flight guard lives **inside** `handleSubmitNonAdaptive` (and\r\n  225:   stays inside `commitCurrentAdaptiveAnswer`). Shape:\r\n  226:   `pendingCommitsRef: Map<qid, Promise<Map<string, Attempt> | null>>`. On\r\n  227:   entry, if the map has a promise for the qid, **return that promise**;\r\n  228:   otherwise create it, store it, delete it in `finally`. A\r\n  229:   `pendingCommitQid: string | null` state, set beside the map, disables the\r\n  230:   arrows, the ovals and End while a commit is in flight.\r\n  231: - Test: double right-arrow, and right-arrow then End, with a delayed\r\n  232:   `persistAttempt` mock: exactly one `attempts` insert each.\r\n  233: \r\n  234: ### 7. Argument Breakdown under the stimulus\r\n  235: \r\n  236: - New `src/components/drill/official/ArgumentBreakdown.tsx`, props\r\n  237:   `{ data, isLoading, isError, activeStyle, onStyleChange, fontScale }`.\r\n  238:   A `border-t` rule, the style pills only when `styles.length > 1`, then\r\n  239:   labelled steps (label on its own line, slate-700 600-weight ~0.85 x base;\r\n  240:   body sans at base size, 1.6 line-height).\r\n  241: - Shared helper `resolveBreakdownSteps(data, activeStyle)` in\r\n  242:   `useLrExplanation.ts` carries the borrow-Standard rule; both\r\n  243:   `DrillExplanationTab` and `ArgumentBreakdown` call it.\r\n  244: - `OfficialQuestionPane` gets `belowStimulus?: React.ReactNode`. The slot is\r\n  245:   a **sibling** of the stimulus `div`, inside a new left-column wrapper\r\n  246:   `flex flex-col overflow-y-auto`; the stimulus `div` loses only its own\r\n  247:   `overflow-y-auto` and keeps every other class: serif,\r\n  248:   `whitespace-pre-wrap`, `[&>*]:max-w-[52ch]`, padding, highlight handlers.\r\n  249:   The slot has its own `mt-6 px-6 sm:pl-9 sm:pr-8 font-sans\r\n  250:   whitespace-normal max-w-[60ch]`.\r\n  251: - `Drill.tsx` passes `belowStimulus={explanationOpen ? <ArgumentBreakdown ./> : null}`.\r\n  252: - The drawer\'s Explanation tab stays, rendered from the same data.\r\n  253: \r\n  254: ### 8. Choice Notes as dropdowns\r\n  255: \r\n  256: - `InlineChoiceExplanation` gains `open: boolean` and `onToggle: () => void`.\r\n  257:   Header row (tag chip, "% of students", "Your answer") always renders when\r\n  258:   any of the three exists. The chevron (`ChevronUp`/`ChevronDown`,\r\n  259:   `aria-expanded`, "Hide explanation for A" / "Show explanation for A") and\r\n  260:   the prose render **only when `note.trim().length > 0`**. A selected\r\n  261:   choice with no published note gets its "Your answer" chip and no\r\n  262:   dropdown; `isInlineChoiceEmpty` keeps its job.\r\n  263: - The choice grid gains a 4th column so the chevron aligns under the\r\n  264:   Check/X icon column (shared with the Restore button from step 3).\r\n  265: - `OfficialQuestionPane` owns `openLetters: Set<string>`, reset to every\r\n  266:   letter with a trimmed note whenever `inlineNotes` becomes non-null or the\r\n  267:   qid changes (pressing Explanation always opens expanded, per the\r\n  268:   recording). Turning Explanation off discards it.\r\n  269: - The "ALWAYS VISIBLE" comment at the top of `InlineChoiceExplanation` is\r\n  270:   rewritten to state the new rule and why.\r\n  271: \r\n  272: ### 9. Tooltips on the pills\r\n  273: \r\n  274: `OfficialSessionStrip`: Answer `title` = "Show correct answer" / "Hide\r\n  275: correct answer"; Explanation `title` = "Show explanations" / "Hide\r\n  276: explanations". Copy from the LSAT Lab recording.\r\n  277: \r\n  278: ### 10. Census of questions with no authored explanation\r\n  279: \r\n  280: - `scripts/missing-explanations-census.mjs`. Loads the service key the way\r\n  281:   `scripts/db-pull.mjs` does, and **pages every table at 1000 rows with\r\n  282:   `Range` headers until a short page**, the same loop `db-pull.mjs` uses.\r\n  283:   Aborts (non-zero exit, nothing written) on any query error or if a table\'s\r\n  284:   row count disagrees with a `count=exact` head request.\r\n  285: - Reads the bank from `public/data/PT*-LR.json` (156 files).\r\n  286: - Shared predicate: a question **has authored prose** iff at least one\r\n  287:   `lr_explanation_steps.body` or `lr_explanation_choice_notes.body` for it is\r\n  288:   non-empty after `trim()`. The same trimmed rule replaces the current\r\n  289:   `hasAuthored` in `useLrExplanation.ts` (which counts rows, not prose).\r\n  290: - Reasons are exactly what the data proves: `not ingested` (no\r\n  291:   `lr_questions` row for the address) or `no authored prose` (row exists,\r\n  292:   nothing passes the predicate). No "video only" label: nothing in the\r\n  293:   schema records a video; the 14 known video-only questions (PT143, PT146\r\n  294:   per the 2026-08-03 migration) are a subset of "no authored prose", noted\r\n  295:   in the report header.\r\n  296: - Output `ai-os/wiki/explanations/MISSING-EXPLANATIONS.md`: summary counts\r\n  297:   (bank total, ingested, with prose, missing by reason), then one table per\r\n  298:   PrepTest, rows `PT � S � Q � question type � reason`, sorted PT, section,\r\n  299:   question. []).map((c) => ({\r\n153:    letter: c.letter,\r\n154:    text: c.choice_text,\r\n155:    tag: c.tag,\r\n156:    studentPercent: c.student_percent,\r\n157:    isCorrect: c.is_correct,\r\n158:  }));\r\n159:\r\n160:  const hasAuthored = styles.some(\r\n161:    (s) => s.steps.length > 0 || Object.keys(s.notesByLetter).length > 0,\r\n162:  );\r\n163:\r\n164:  return {\r\n165:    questionId: question.id,\r\n166:    preptest: question.preptest,\r\n167:    section: question.section,\r\n168:    questionNumber: question.question_number,\r\n169:    questionType: question.question_type,\r\n170:    correctChoice: question.correct_choice,\r\n171:    choices,\r\n172:    styles,\r\n173:    hasAuthored,\r\n174:  };\r\n175:}\r\n176:\r\n177:export function useLrExplanation(query: LrExplanationQuery, enabled = true) {\r\n178:  const { pt, section, questionNumber } = query;\r\n179:  return useQuery({\r\n180:    queryKey: [\'lr-explanation\', pt, section, questionNumber],\r\n181:    queryFn: () => fetchLrExplanation(query),\r\n182:    enabled: enabled && pt != null && section != null && questionNumber != null,\r\n183:    staleTime: Infinity, // authored content is immutable between ingests\r\n184:  });\r\n185:}\r\n### src/components/drill/official/OfficialAnswerNavigator.tsx\r\n1:import * as React from "react";\r\n2:import { ArrowLeft, ArrowRight, ClipboardCheck } from "lucide-react";\r\n3:import { Switch } from "@/components/ui/switch";\r\n4:import {\r\n5:  Tooltip,\r\n6:  TooltipContent,\r\n7:  TooltipProvider,\r\n8:  TooltipTrigger,\r\n9:} from "@/components/ui/tooltip";\r\n10:import { cn } from "@/lib/utils";\r\n11:import AnswerOvals from "@/components/drill/official/AnswerOvals";\r\n12:\r\n13:interface Props {\r\n14:  /** Ordered list of qids in the current session. */\r\n15:  questionQueue: string[];\r\n16:  /** Current 0-based index. */\r\n17:  currentIndex: number;\r\n18:  /** Set of qids with at least one recorded attempt. Leaving the section runs through Complete Section in\r\n 4224:             // the header strip.\r\n 4225:             <OfficialAnswerNavigator\r\n 4226:               questionQueue={session.questionQueue}\r\n 4227:               currentIndex={session.currentIndex}\r\n 4228:               attemptedQids={attemptedQids}\r\n 4229:               jumpEnabled={jumpEnabled}\r\n 4230:               jumpDisabledReason={timer?.isPaused ? \'Paused - resume to navigate\' : undefined}\r\n 4231:               showQuestionList\r\n 4232:               prevDisabled={session.currentIndex <= 0 || !!timer?.isPaused}\r\n 4233:               nextDisabled={\r\n 4234:                 officialNextDisabled ||\r\n 4235:                 session.currentIndex >= session.questionQueue.length - 1\r\n 4236:               }\r\n 4237:               nextDisabledReason={\r\n 4238:                 timer?.isPaused\r\n 4239:                   ? \'Paused - resume to navigate\'\r\n 4240:                   : session.currentIndex >= session.questionQueue.length - 1\r\n 4241:                   ? \'Last question - use Complete Section to finish\'\r\n 4242:                   : undefined\r\n 4243:               }\r\n 4244:               onJumpTo={handleJumpTo}\r\n 4245:               onPrev={officialPrev}\r\n 4246:               onNext={() => { void officialNext(); }}\r\n 4247:               revealEnabled={revealSealed ? false : sectionRevealEnabled}\r\n 4248:               onRevealEnabledChange={revealSealed ? undefined : setSectionRevealEnabled}\r\n 4249:             />\r\n 4250:           ) : (\r\n 4251:             // Same chrome as the adaptive Smart Drill, but driven by the fixed\r\n 4252:             // queue the student hand-picked from the Smart LR Drill card. Submit\r\n 4253:             // saves and reveals in place (honoring the auto-reveal toggle); Next\r\n 4254:             // walks the queue via the shared officialNext; Skip advances past the\r\n 4255:             // answer gate; End finishes the section.\r\n 4256:             <OfficialSmartDrillBar\r\n 4257:               autoReveal={autoReveal}\r\n 4258:               onAutoRevealChange={handleAutoRevealChange}\r\n 4259:               primaryAction={currentAttempted || currentUnscored ? \'next\' : \'submit\'}\r\n 4260:               primaryDisabled={!selectedAnswer || !!timer?.isPaused}\r\n 4261:               onSubmit={() => {\r\n 4262:                 void (async () => {\r\n 4263:                   const submitted = selectedAnswer;\r\n 4264:                   await handleSubmitNonAdaptive(submitted);\r\n 4265:                   if (autoReveal) {\r\n 4266:                     setShowAnswerRevealed(prev => new Map(prev).set(currentQuestion.qid, true));\r\n 4267:                   }\r\n 4268:                   // Pop the AI tutor (post-answer when revealed) on a wrong answer\r\n 4269:                   // so the student gets a specific, back-and-forth explanation\r\n 4270:                   // instead of the old dead "ANALYSIS VALID" placeholder.\r\n 4271:                   const tutorLaunch = resolveTutorLaunch({\r\n 4272:                     tutorModeEnabled: tutorMode,\r\n 4273:                     correct: submitted === currentQuestion.correctAnswer,\r\n 4274:                     answerRevealed: autoReveal,\r\n 4275:                   });\r\n 4276:                   if (tutorLaunch.shouldOpen) {\r\n 4277:                     setAnswerLocked(true);\r\n 4278:                     setTutorQuestionSnapshot(currentQuestion);\r\n 4279:                     setTutorUserAnswerSnapshot(submitted);\r\n 4280:                     setTutorChatMode(tutorLaunch.mode);\r\n 4281:                     setTutorChatOpen(true);\r\n 4282:                   }\r\n 4283:                 })();\r\n 4284:               }}\r\n 4285:               onNext={() => { void officialNext(); }}\r\n 4286:               // A built set is a fixed list, so it walks both ways. Skip only\r\n 4287:               // ever went forward, and only before the question was answered,\r\n 4288:               // which left no way back to a question already worked - the one\r\n 4289:               // thing reviewing a set is for.\r\n 4290:               onPrev={() => {\r\n 4291:                 void (async () => {\r\n 4292:                   await commitPendingSelection();\r\n 4293:                   officialPrev();\r\n 4294:                 })();\r\n 4295:               }}\r\n 4296:               prevDisabled={session.currentIndex <= 0 || !!timer?.isPaused}\r\n 4297:               onAdvance={() => {\r\n 4298:                 void (async () => {\r\n 4299:                   await commitPendingSelection();\r\n 4300:                   advanceInQueue();\r\n 4301:                 })();\r\n 4302:               }}\r\n 4303:               advanceDisabled={!!timer?.isPaused}\r\n 4304:               // Submit is only ever the commit action now. Once the question is\r\n 4305:               // answered (or deliberately revealed unscored) the Next pill is\r\n### ADR 0011\r\n1:# 0011 — An oval is a question, not a verdict\r\n2:\r\n3:Date: 2026-08-31\r\n4:Status: Accepted\r\n5:\r\n6:## Context\r\n7:\r\n8:A student who builds their own LR set could only move forward through it. A full\r\n9:section has always had the LawHub bottom strip — one oval per question, click any\r\n10:of them to jump — and a built set had nothing equivalent, so the questions the\r\n11:student chose could only be walked in the order they came out.\r\n12:\r\n13:Two things were actually wrong, and only one of them was missing work:\r\n14:\r\n15:1. `Drill.tsx` had been passing `onPrev` and `onAdvance` to `OfficialSmartDrillBar`\r\n16:   for some time, but the bar never rendered those pills. The handlers were\r\n17:   received and dropped. This was a bug, not a gap.\r\n18:2. The oval strip genuinely did not exist for a built set.\r\n19:\r\n20:Fixing (2) raised a question a section never had to answer. A section never\r\n21:reveals, so its ovals could only ever mean "answered". A built set *does* reveal:\r\n22:the moment a question is revealed the app knows whether the student got it right,\r\n23:and that information could be shown on the oval — green for correct, red for\r\n24:missed, jump straight to your misses.\r\n25:\r\n26:## Decision\r\n27:\r\n28:The strip stays exactly as rich as the section\'s. An oval carries three states and\r\n29:no more: **current**, **answered**, **jumpable**. It never shows whether the answer\r\n30:was right, in a set or anywhere else.\r\n31:\r\n32:The reason is what an oval *is*. One oval stands for one **question** — a place to\r\n33:go. It does not stand for an answer choice, even though on paper the bubble you\r\n34:fill in is precisely your answer. Colouring ovals by correctness quietly recasts a\r\n35:navigation control as a scorecard, and then the strip is doing two jobs and reads\r\n36:as neither.\r\n37:\r\n38:The strip is extracted into `AnswerOvals` so the section and the built set render\r\n39:one component rather than two copies that drift apart.\r\n40:\r\n41:Jumping commits any pending selection before it moves, for the same reason\r\n42:Previous does: leaving a question must never be the thing that loses the answer on\r\n43:it.\r\n44:\r\n45:## Consequences\r\n46:\r\n47:- A built set now navigates like a section: ovals, Previous, Next, free movement.\r\n48:- Wanting a right/wrong overview after a set is a real want, and it now has an\r\n49:  obvious home that is *not* the ovals — the post-set review screen, where being a\r\n50:  scorecard is the job.\r\n51:- `AnswerOvals` is shared, so a change to oval appearance lands in both surfaces at\r\n52:  once. That is the point, and it also means neither surface can quietly diverge.\r\n53:- Rejected: colouring on reveal, and colouring only after the set ends. Both were\r\n54:  offered and both were declined on the grounds above.\r\n### CONTEXT\r\n1:# Guided Drills & Explanations\r\n2:\r\n3:The practice domain: how LSAT U presents drills, the questions inside them, and the teaching that wraps each one. Covers the drill surface (`src/components/drill/`, `src/pages/Drill.tsx`), the question bank (`src/lib/questionLoader.ts`, `public/data/PT*-LR.json`), and the guided-drill catalog (`src/lib/guidedDrills.ts`, `src/data/guidedDrillHtmlManifest.ts`).\r\n4:\r\n5:## Language\r\n6:\r\n7:**Question Explanation**:\r\n8:The question-level teaching shown *after* a student answers one specific question. In the schema this is the `breakdown` (conclusion, conclusionSimple, evidence, justification, objection, prediction, crucialInsight) plus `answerChoiceExplanations` (per-choice verdict + whyCorrect/whyIncorrect) on an `LRQuestion`. One per question. Currently authored for **0 of 3,964** questions — this is the headline gap (Phase 2).\r\n9:_Avoid_: explanation (ambiguous), breakdown (that\'s only one half), answer key, rationale.\r\n10:\r\n11:**Drill Instruction Card**:\r\n12:The drill-level teaching panel shown *before* a student starts a guided drill — teaches the pattern the drill targets ("these six questions all hide the conclusion in the first sentence..."). One per guided drill. Stored as `instruction_card: { left_md, right_md }` on a guided drill. The LSAT Lab equivalent is its "Info" card (captured verbatim in `[[LSAT-LAB-LR-INFO-CARDS]]` in the vault).\r\n13:_Avoid_: info card (that\'s LSAT Lab\'s name), intro, launch card, explanation.\r\n14:\r\n15:**Question Type**:\r\n16:The LSAT task a question asks — *Main Conclusion, Flaw, Necessary Assumption, Strengthen, Paradox, Must Be True*, etc. Stored as `questionType` on a question (and `question_types[]` on a guided drill). The bank\'s raw values are messy (110 variants, casing dupes, 67 "Unknown") and need a canonical set (~25 types).\r\n17:_Avoid_: qtype in prose (fine in code), category (that\'s Patterns/Process), question kind.\r\n18:\r\n19:**Question Feature**:\r\n20:A finer-grained pattern tag layered on top of the type — *First Sentence Conclusion, Explain Curious Fact, Anti-Causal, Famous Flaws, Conditional Diagramming, Missing Link / Idea*. Stored as `questionFeatures[]`. 60 clean values; this vocabulary effectively defines the guided-drill taxonomy.\r\n21:_Avoid_: tag, pattern (overloaded), reasoning type (that\'s a separate field).\r\n22:\r\n23:**Reasoning Type**:\r\n24:The underlying logical structure — *Causation, Conditional Logic, Comparisons*. Stored as `reasoningType[]`. Distinct from Question Feature.\r\n25:_Avoid_: using interchangeably with Question Feature.\r\n26:\r\n27:**Drill Signature** (proposed):\r\n28:The accurate definition of what a guided drill drills = the set of Question Types + Question Features of the *actual questions in its pool* (its qids), NOT the types/features merely mentioned in its Drill Instruction Card text.\r\n29:_Avoid_: deriving a drill\'s targets from card prose alone (it over-captures).\r\n30:\r\n31:**Guided Drill**:\r\n32:A curated, fixed practice set of LSAT questions with a Drill Instruction Card and (target state) a deterministic ordered question list. 108 exist (`guidedDrillHtmlManifest.ts`).\r\n33:_Avoid_: drill (too generic — could mean Smart Drill), set, assignment.\r\n34:\r\n35:**Answer Navigator** (the "bubbles"):\r\n36:The strip along the bottom of the official testing frame: one small empty **oval**\r\n37:per question in the queue, the question number printed *below* each oval, the\r\n38:current question\'s number bold with a thin I-beam tick above it. Rendered by\r\n39:`OfficialAnswerNavigator`. Joshua calls the ovals "bubbles"; the code calls them\r\n40:ovals and the strip an answer navigator. Both names mean this one thing.\r\n41:One oval stands for one **question**, never for an answer choice. This matters\r\n42:because "bubble" is overloaded: on paper, the bubble you fill in IS your answer\r\n43:(A-E). Here it is not. An oval is a place to go, so anything that turns it into a\r\n44:verdict on your answer is out of scope for it by definition.\r\n45:_Avoid_: pager (that is the RC per-question control that lives in the bar\'s\r\n46:`pager` slot), dots, progress bar (it is navigation, not a progress readout),\r\n47:and using "bubble" for an A-E answer choice.\r\n48:\r\n49:**Oval State**:\r\n50:What one oval in the Answer Navigator communicates. Today there are exactly three,\r\n51:and they are orthogonal: **current** (which question you are on), **answered** (a\r\n52:qid present in `attemptedQids`, meaning at least one recorded attempt), and\r\n53:**jumpable** (clicking moves you there, governed by `jumpEnabled`). Notably there\r\n54:is no correct/incorrect state: a section never reveals, so the strip has never had\r\n55:to say whether an answer was right.\r\n56:_Avoid_: "completed" or "done" (answered does not mean finished or correct),\r\n57:"selected" (that is an answer choice, not an oval).\r\n58:\r\n59:**Drill Launch**:\r\n60:Everything the runner needs to be told in order to start a drill: which kind of\r\n61:drill, and the name of the particular run. It is carried in the URL and nowhere\r\n62:else, so any drill can be refreshed, bookmarked, or reopened. A Drill Launch\r\n63:names a run; it does not contain one. The questions, the answers already given\r\n64:and the position in the queue live in the Run Record.\r\n65:_Avoid_: route state, config (that word currently means two different things in\r\n66:the code, a filter and a fixed question list), params, drill options.\r\n67:\r\n68:**Drill Run Spec**:\r\n69:A Drill Launch after its row has been read: the mode, the question list or the\r\n70:filter that draws one, and the ids saying which stored row this run belongs to.\r\n71:Built by `drillRunSpecFromLaunch` and handed to the runner through context by\r\n72:`DrillLaunchGate`, which is the only way the runner learns what it is running.\r\n73:The Drill Launch is what a link says; the Drill Run Spec is what the runner is\r\n74:told. One is written and read from a URL, the other never touches one.\r\n75:_Avoid_: route state (the shape this replaced, and it read the URL a second\r\n76:time), drill config (that word already means a filter or a fixed list), session.\r\n77:\r\n78:**Run Record**:\r\n79:The durable row, per student per run, holding what a run actually consists of:\r\n80:the ordered question list, every attempt, the position, and the clock already\r\n81:spent. Survives a closed tab, a sign-out, and a different device. It is the\r\n82:reason a Drill Launch can be short: the launch names the run and the Run Record\r\n83:supplies its contents. A named generated run is keyed by that name, such as a\r\n84:Bootcamp day and part or a Guided drill id, so a fresh draw cannot strand the\r\n85:record behind a different question list.\r\n86:_Avoid_: resume state (that is what it is for, not what it is), snapshot (that is\r\n87:one write to it), session (overloaded: `DrillSession` is the in-memory object for\r\n88:the current screen, and an auth session is a third thing).\r\n89:\n90:**Answer** (the pill):\n91:The on/off control in the session strip that shows which choice is correct.\n92:On: the correct choice\'s badge goes green, and if the student already submitted\n93:a different choice, that one goes red. Off: both colours leave and the choices\n94:read as untouched. It says nothing about *why*; that is the Explanation pill.\n95:Neither direction locks anything and neither direction touches the logged\n96:attempt. Mirrors LSAT Lab\'s Answer button ("Show correct answer" / hide).\n97:_Avoid_: reveal (the code\'s word; fine in code, but "reveal" sounds one-way and\n98:this is a toggle), solution, show answer.\n99:\n100:**Explanation** (the pill):\n101:The on/off control beside Answer that opens the whole Question Explanation:\n102:the Argument Breakdown under the stimulus and in the drawer, and every Choice\n103:Note expanded. Off folds all of it away and leaves the question exactly as it\n104:was, including any Answer colouring already on. Never opens on its own: a wrong\n105:submit does not press it, only the student does. Mirrors LSAT Lab\'s\n106:Explanation button ("Hide explanations").\n107:_Avoid_: breakdown (that is one half of what it opens), tutor, coach.\n108:\n109:**Argument Breakdown**:\n110:The question-level half of a Question Explanation: the ordered labelled steps\n111:(Background, Evidence, Conclusion, Evaluate, Goal, and so on) plus the style\n112:pills (Standard / Brief / Another Way) when the source published more than one\n113:style. Lives under the stimulus while Explanation is on, and is repeated in the\n114:drawer\'s Explanation tab.\n115:_Avoid_: explanation (the pill), breakdown steps, analysis.\n116:\n117:**Choice Note**:\n118:The per-choice half of a Question Explanation: one choice\'s tag ("Out of\n119:Scope", "Premise"), its "% of students", and its prose. Rendered as a dropdown\n120:under that choice, open by default while Explanation is on, with a chevron the\n121:student can fold and reopen per choice. A choice with nothing published gets\n122:no dropdown at all, never an empty one.\n123:_Avoid_: inline explanation (the component\'s old name), answer explanation,\n124:rationale.\n125:\n126:**Reset Response**:\n127:The control under the choices that clears the student\'s pick and nothing else.\n128:The timer keeps running, the Answer and Explanation pills keep their state, and\n129:an attempt already logged stays logged. Greyed out until a choice is picked.\n130:A pick made after a Reset on an already-attempted question is practice, not a\n131:second attempt. Mirrors LSAT Lab\'s Reset Response button.\n132:_Avoid_: undo, clear, retry (retry implies a new scored attempt).\n133:\n134:**AI Tutor**:\n135:The generated coaching surfaces on the drill screen: the Coach chat that used to\n136:open after a wrong answer, the "Ask Joshua about this question" hand-off on a\n137:question with no authored prose, and the "Explain your reasoning" voice chip.\n138:Distinct from the Question Explanation, which is transcribed LSAT Lab content\n139:and never generated. Off for every student as of 2026-09-12 behind one switch;\n140:the code stays so it can be turned back on.\n141:_Avoid_: tutor mode (the code\'s flag name), Joshua (the chat\'s persona; a\n142:student reading "Joshua is having trouble" thinks the person is), coach (one of\n143:its tabs).\n'}]

## Tool: exec

const r = await tools.exec_command({cmd: "$p=Get-Content 'PLAN-post-question-explanation.md'; for($i=296;$i -le $p.Count;$i++){'{0,4}: {1}' -f $i,$p[$i-1]}; $d=Get-Content 'src/pages/Drill.tsx'; foreach($range in @(@(3530,3650),@(3750,3890),@(3980,4198),@(4295,4335))){Write-Output (\"### {0}-{1}\" -f $range[0],$range[1]);for($i=$range[0];$i -le $range[1];$i++){'{0,5}: {1}' -f $i,$d[$i-1]}}", workdir: "C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os\\projects\\lsatu", yield_time_ms: 10000, max_output_tokens: 30000}); text(r.output);

Run once, committed to the vault, not the app repo.\r\n 300: \r\n 301: ### 11. Tests\r\n 302: \r\n 303: - `OfficialQuestionPane.test.tsx`: the four Answer x Explanation\r\n 304:   combinations; Explanation on \x1a every noted letter expanded; chevron folds\r\n 305:   one; new qid re-expands; a selected choice with no note shows the chip\r\n 306:   and no chevron; choices stay clickable while `answerRevealed` when\r\n 307:   `choicesStayLive`; eliminated correct choice has a working Restore; Reset\r\n 308:   Response disabled with no pick, clears the pick, leaves `answerRevealed`\r\n 309:   alone.\r\n 310: - `InlineChoiceExplanation`: closed renders header, no prose; whitespace\r\n 311:   note renders no chevron.\r\n 312: - `tutorBehavior.test.ts` (new): `resolveTutorLaunch` with\r\n 313:   `flagEnabled=false` never opens, whatever `tutorModeEnabled` is.\r\n 314: - Drill-level, fixed drill: bottom bar is the Answer Navigator, no Submit;\r\n 315:   right arrow records the pick; Answer pill with a pick records it then\r\n 316:   colours; Answer pill with no pick opens the confirm, Cancel changes\r\n 317:   nothing; Explanation same; failed save leaves the question in place and\r\n 318:   writes nothing; Reset then a new pick writes no attempt; double\r\n 319:   right-arrow persists once; no tutor after a wrong pick.\r\n 320: - Drill-level, full section and practice set: both pills behave as before.\r\n 321: - Existing tests asserting the tutor opens after a wrong answer, or that a\r\n 322:   fixed queue renders a Submit pill, are updated.\r\n 323: \r\n 324: ### 12. Verify in the browser\r\n 325: \r\n 326: Preview server, at 1280, 1024 and 390 widths and with the drawer docked:\r\n 327: - A guided drill: bottom bar is arrows + ovals, no Submit; pick B, press\r\n 328:   Answer \x1a B red, correct green, nothing else; Answer again \x1a colours gone;\r\n 329:   Explanation \x1a breakdown under stimulus, dropdowns open; fold one;\r\n 330:   Explanation off \x1a dropdowns and breakdown gone; right arrow \x1a oval marks\r\n 331:   answered; back arrow \x1a pick and states as left; Reset Response \x1a pick\r\n 332:   cleared, timer running; pick again \x1a `attempts` row count for that user +\r\n 333:   qid **unchanged** from a count taken just before Reset; End Drill from\r\n 334:   the strip closes out.\r\n 335: - Smart Drill: its own bar unchanged (Submit, Skip, auto-reveal switch);\r\n 336:   pills, breakdown, dropdowns and Reset work; no tutor after a wrong\r\n 337:   Submit.\r\n 338: - Cold cache first open: with an empty React Query cache, press Explanation\r\n 339:   on a fresh question; the slot shows "Loading explanation." then the\r\n 340:   content, with no layout jump of the stimulus block.\r\n 341: - A sealed run, a full section and a practice set: selection and\r\n 342:   navigation unchanged.\r\n 343: - Computed style of the stimulus `div` (font-family, font-size, padding,\r\n 344:   max-width) identical before and after, read with `getComputedStyle`.\r\n 345: - Screenshots for Joshua.\r\n 346: \r\n 347: ## Key decisions & tradeoffs\r\n 348: \r\n 349: - **Fixed drills use the section\'s bar.** Joshua\'s ask, and it removes a\r\n 350:   whole submit path plus the auto-reveal question rather than adjudicating\r\n 351:   it. The pick-on-arrow commit already exists and is reused.\r\n 352: - **A pick present when a pill is pressed is committed silently.**\r\n 353:   Assumption, stated in step 2. The alternative (asking "unscored?" about a\r\n 354:   visibly answered question) is worse in every case.\r\n 355: - **Flag gates mounting, not just state.** Round 1 showed the state-only\r\n 356:   version leaked through the legacy controls.\r\n 357: - **Answer and Explanation decoupled at the state level**, only in the\r\n 358:   `reviewControlsAvailable` modes, with today\'s code as the else branch.\r\n 359: - **Practice state is a map with an explicit empty pick.** A cleared\r\n 360:   practice response and "never touched" must be different values or Reset\r\n 361:   restores the recorded answer.\r\n 362: - **Failures stop the caller chain.** A null from the commit function is\r\n 363:   only honest if nothing downstream ignores it.\r\n 364: - **Pane changes are opt-in props.** `choicesStayLive`, `belowStimulus`,\r\n 365:   `onResetResponse` all default to today\'s behaviour.\r\n 366: - **Dropdowns open by default, chevron only with prose.**\r\n 367: - **Census says only what the data proves.**\r\n 368: - **Flag, not deletion** of the tutor. Joshua wants it back later.\r\n 369: \r\n 370: ## Risks / open questions\r\n 371: \r\n 372: - `officialPrev` / `handleJumpTo` may not commit a pending pick today; the\r\n 373:   audit in step 3 settles it.\r\n 374: - `showSolution` / `showReviewButton` remain referenced by the legacy\r\n 375:   layout; the audit may find the official surface still reads them.\r\n 376: - Breakdown label sizing vs LSAT Lab is a taste call; verify on screen.\r\n 377: - The census needs the service key from `~/ai-os/.env`; if absent it says\r\n 378:   so and exits non-zero.\r\n 379: \r\n 380: ## Out of scope\r\n 381: \r\n 382: - Smart Drill\'s question selection bug and its bottom bar.\r\n 383: - `TutorChatModal`, the edge function, token budgeting.\r\n 384: - Sealed runs, practice-test sittings, full-section mode, Bootcamp practice\r\n 385:   sets, the classic `DrillTopBar` layout: behaviour preserved.\r\n 386: - Reading Comprehension (`RcChoiceDisclosure`) and the Classroom\r\n 387:   `LRQuestionModal`.\r\n 388: - Video explanations: no toggle, no hosting, no claim.\r\n 389: - Authoring the missing explanations.\r\n### 3530-3650\r\n 3530:   // built set needs exactly this - showing the answer used to spend the\r\n 3531:   // question for good.\r\n 3532:   //\r\n 3533:   // Only the view is undone. [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 0.8 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': "# 0016 - A drill link names a run, it does not contain one\r\n\r\nDate: 2026-09-01\r\nStatus: Accepted\r\n\r\n## Context\r\n\r\nSeventeen places start a drill. Nine build a URL; eight hand the drill to\r\n`/drill` in `location.state` and put nothing in the address bar. Refresh one of\r\nthose eight and the drill is gone: a saved set, a Smart Drill, a Testing Centre\r\nsection. `ptRun` is worse than gone - it never went through the resolver at all\r\n(`Drill.tsx:202`), so a refreshed practice-test leg loses the run id it needs to\r\nwrite its close-out, and that section can never be marked finished.\r\n\r\n`getResolvedDrillRouteState` reads both transports into one shape but only one\r\nway. Nothing turns a launch back into a URL, so the URL grammar is whatever each\r\ncaller happened to build, and the two can disagree: several links carry an\r\nassignment id *and* a copy of that assignment's question list.\r\n\r\nThe obvious fix is to put the questions in the URL. That is the wrong fix. There\r\nis already a durable per-student Run Record holding the ordered question list,\r\nevery attempt, the position and the clock spent, and on resume the runner adopts\r\nthat stored queue whole rather than re-shuffling (`Drill.tsx:786`). Refresh\r\nalready returns the identical run. The only thing refresh destroys is the app's\r\nability to work out *which run the student was in*.\r\n\r\nSeparately, `mode` is not the kind. Six materially different things all launch as\r\n`mode=type-drill`, told apart only by which extra flag rides along\r\n(`guidedAssignmentId`, `assignmentId`, `guidedSetId`, `practiceSlug`, `present`).\r\n\r\n## Decision\r\n\r\nA **Drill Launch** is carried in the URL and nowhere else, and it carries the\r\n*name* of a run, not its contents. `location.state` stops being a transport.\r\n\r\nThe launch is one discriminated union over nine kinds: Smart Drill, Section,\r\nSaved set, Assignment, Guided drill, Practice set, Bootcamp day, Questions (an\r\nad-hoc list), and Filter. A practice-test leg is a **Section with a run\r\nattached**, not its own kind: it runs, looks and closes out identically, and the\r\nonly difference is that something is waiting on the result.\r\n\r\nAnything that has an id is named by that id alone. `?set=abc123` and not the six\r\nqids inside it, so editing a set cannot leave an old link running last week's\r\nquestions. Only the ad-hoc list and the filter put a payload in the link, because\r\nthey have no row to point at. Guided drills and assignments already resolve their\r\nown questions (`guidedDrills.ts:82`), so nothing is lost.\r\n\r\nSwitches are split rather than pooled: the cross-cutting ones (back link,\r\nheading, which question to open on) sit alongside the kind, and the rest live\r\ninside the kind that owns them (sealed and timing on a Section, duration on a\r\nGuided drill). A switch that does not belong to its kind is **rejected** when it\r\nwould change what the student experiences, and ignored when it is decoration. A\r\nsealed section that silently is not sealed is a broken measurement; a missing\r\nheading is not.\r\n\r\n`launchDrill()` and `parseDrillLaunch()` are a matched pair and round-trip.\r\n\r\n## Consequences\r\n\r\n- A dead link (deleted assignment, deleted set, another student's set) shows an\r\n  error with a way back, rather than falling back to a generic drill. A student\r\n  quietly drilling something nobody assigned is worse than a student reporting a\r\n  broken link.\r\n- An id-only link costs one row read before the drill can start, and it is the\r\n  read that makes the link correct.\r\n- The drill page is not rewritten. A small converter hands it the shape it\r\n  already expects, so the fix lands as a diff that can be read in one sitting.\r\n  The converter is temporary and says so.\r\n- `/drill` canonicalizes its own URL on arrival, which covers any launch path\r\n  missed here. Two buttons still change, because they discard identity the page\r\n  cannot recover: saved sets throw away the set id (`SavedSets.tsx:39`), and the\r\n  bootcamp day button hands over picked questions without saying it was Day 3\r\n  homework (`TodayView.tsx:112`).\r\n- Today's URL grammar is kept as an explicitly-marked legacy branch of the\r\n  parser, and never written. Assigned sections go out as links that sit in a\r\n  student's inbox for weeks; breaking one is a support message from a student.\r\n  The branch is deletable once those links have aged out.\r\n- Rejected: putting the resolved question list in every URL. It duplicates the\r\n  Run Record, produces links too long to send, and would have made a filter\r\n  drill's identity the questions it happened to draw rather than what the\r\n  student asked for.\r\n\r\n## When a kind can be named by its id\r\n\r\nFound while building this, and it is the constraint that decides the remaining\r\nwork. Naming a run by an id is safe only when one of these holds:\r\n\r\n1. **Re-resolving the id yields the same questions.** A saved set and a\r\n   classroom assignment both store an explicit list, so re-reading the row on\r\n   refresh returns what the student is part-way through.\r\n2. **The Run Record is keyed by that id rather than by the question list.** A\r\n   guided drill launched from an assignment is keyed `guided::<assignmentId>`,\r\n   so even a different draw finds the right row and adopts its stored queue.\r\n\r\nWhere neither holds, an id-named link is actively worse than a long one: the\r\nrunner re-resolves, draws a different list, computes a different Run Record key\r\nfrom that list, finds no row, and the student loses a drill they were half way\r\nthrough. That rules out, for now:\r\n\r\n- **A bootcamp day.** Its questions come through a per-student recently-seen\r\n  filter, and its Run Record is keyed by the resulting list.\r\n- **A guided drill with no assignment.** It resolves through a per-student level\r\n  band, and without an assignment id it falls back to the same list-keyed Run\r\n  Record.\r\n\r\nBoth become safe the moment their Run Record is keyed by the name in the link -\r\nthe day, or the drill - which is a separate change and the natural next one.\r\n\r\n## Follow-up: generated runs are keyed by their name\r\n\r\nImplemented 2026-09-03. A Bootcamp day now writes its Run Record under the day\r\nand part, and an assignment-less Guided drill writes under its drill id. Reads\r\ntry that identity key first and consult the old question-list or guided-drill\r\nslug key only when the identity-keyed row does not exist. A completed identity\r\nrow therefore cannot resurrect an older in-progress run. The next snapshot\r\nwrites only the identity key, so an old active run migrates forward without a\r\nbulk data rewrite.\r\n\r\n## Follow-up: the runner reads the launch\r\n\r\nImplemented 2026-09-03. The temporary converter is gone, and with it the second\r\ngrammar. `drillRouteState` was a parallel reader of the same query string, and\r\nit agreed with the codec only by inspection. The disagreements were latent\r\nrather than live: `?mode=full-section&timer=70` kept its accommodated clock,\r\nbecause the reader that read `timer` was the one the runner used and the reader\r\nthat dropped it was only ever asked about canonical links. Nothing was broken.\r\nThe point is that nothing would have told us when that stopped being true - and\r\nswitching the runner onto the other reader is exactly the change that would have\r\nmade it true. Every such disagreement was closed before the switch, which is\r\nwhat the golden table of older links in `drillLaunch.test.ts` now holds in\r\nplace.\r\n\r\n`DrillLaunchGate` now resolves the launch and hands `Drill.tsx` a **Drill Run\r\nSpec** through context. The runner no longer reads the query string for anything\r\nabout the run: not the mode, not the assignment id, not the bootcamp day. There\r\nis one reader (`parseDrillLaunch`) and one shape (`drillRunSpecFromLaunch`).\r\n\r\nThe visible change is that a short link stays short. Previously the gate\r\nresolved `?drill=saved-set&set=.` and immediately replaced the address bar with\r\nthe expanded question list, which threw away the naming the whole ADR is about:\r\nthe first refresh was already replaying a frozen list. Now the link the student\r\nwas sent is the link they keep, and each refresh re-reads the row.\r\n\r\nOlder links are read but never rewritten, so everything one says has to be read\r\nin `parseDrillLaunch` - there is no second reader left to pick up what it drops.\r\nThat is why the legacy branch grew: the timer mode, the practice-test leg, the\r\ninitial index, and the identity ids the runner used to read for itself.\r\n\r\nAn in-memory handover is still written into the address bar, now in the\r\ncanonical grammar. What such a handover carries beyond a bare question list -\r\nthe mode, whether it is guided, whether it is sealed - travels as a `RunCarry`\r\nunder its own params, so writing it out and reading it back cannot quietly turn\r\na `natural-drill` into a type-drill.\r\n\r\n"}]

## assistant

1. **Fatal declaration-order bug:** the plan places `reviewControlsAvailable` near [Drill.tsx:215](C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\lsatu\src\pages\Drill.tsx:215), but it reads `session` and `isPracticeSetMode`, whose hooks are declared at lines 307 and 471; this causes a temporal-dead-zone `ReferenceError`.
   Fix: Declare the predicates after line 471 while keeping them above the handlers.

2. **Adaptive scoring can be gamed:** adaptive is included in `reviewControlsAvailable`, but only fixed drills commit a visible pick before Answer/Explanation; a student can reveal the authored answer, change the still-live choice, then Submit a falsely correct adaptive attempt.
   Fix: Atomically commit adaptive picks before either reveal, or require an existing attempt and treat no-attempt reveals as permanently unscored.

3. **The mode classifier violates the domain model:** ADR 0016 explicitly says `mode` is not the run kind—six distinct runs share `type-drill`—yet the predicate treats every non-section/non-adaptive run as a fixed drill.
   Fix: Classify with an exhaustive `DrillRunSpec.kind` switch and explicitly enumerate included and excluded run kinds.

4. **Bootcamp practice can transiently receive forbidden controls:** `isPracticeSetMode` starts as `false` and is established later, so an early render can classify a practice set as fixed despite the plan promising it remains untouched.
   Fix: Derive the policy synchronously from the resolved Drill Run Spec instead of mutable initialization state.

5. **End Drill loses the final pick:** the plan claims `handleFinishSection` commits pending selection, but [Drill.tsx:2545](C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\projects\lsatu\src\pages\Drill.tsx:2545) only consumes `attemptsOverride ?? session.attempts`; it never reads or saves `selectedAnswer`.
   Fix: Add an End wrapper that awaits `commitPendingSelection`, aborts on failure, and passes the returned attempts map to `handleFinishSection`.

6. **`effectiveSelection` is not actually the single source of truth:** the plan says every consumer uses it, then preserves a `commitPendingSelection` guard based on `selectedAnswer`; current next-button disabling also reads `selectedAnswer`.
   Fix: Compute `effectiveAnswer` for the current qid and use it in every guard, disabled prop, pane prop, reset handler, and commit path.

7. **The single-flight design deduplicates writes, not actions:** two rapid arrows can await the same promise and both navigate; arrow followed by End can both continue after the shared save and race navigation against completion.
   Fix: Lock the complete commit-plus-continuation action and test one navigation/completion, not merely one database insert.

8. **The pending-state representation is internally inconsistent:** a `Map<qid, Promise>` permits multiple concurrent qids, while `pendingCommitQid: string | null` represents only one and can re-enable controls while another commit remains pending.
   Fix: Use one global action flight or maintain a reactive `Set` of pending qids.

9. **Choices remain mutable during persistence:** while a delayed commit snapshots answer B, the student can select C or Reset; the eventual attempt records B while the visible state may imply C is the committed/practice response.
   Fix: Disable all response mutations and reveals for that qid during commit, or explicitly queue them as post-commit practice state.

10. **Previous/jump behavior remains ambiguous and easy to double-wrap:** `OfficialAnswerNavigator` invokes its callbacks directly, while current `officialPrev` and `handleJumpTo` do not commit; the plan simultaneously specifies raw callbacks and says they will commit.
    Fix: Introduce one `commitThenNavigate(destination)` wrapper and pass it exactly once for Previous, Next, and oval jumps.

11. **Explanation drawer state can leak across questions:** `explanationOpenByQid` is per-question, but `notesOpen` and `drawerTab` remain global; navigating from open A to closed B can leave the Explanation drawer visible while B’s pill and fetch are off.
    Fix: Synchronize the drawer on qid changes or derive its Explanation-open state from the current qid.

12. **The cross-out invariant is not implementable as written:** the plan says any post-attempt cross-out creates a practice entry, but its only writer runs when selection changes; crossing out an unrelated choice creates no entry.
    Fix: Call `ensurePracticeEntry(qid)` before every post-attempt pick, cross-out, or Reset mutation.

13. **The “borrow Standard” rule is unsupported by the schema:** neither `lr_explanations` nor its child tables records inheritance, so empty Brief/Another Way steps do not prove they are identical to Standard.
    Fix: Verify and document an ingestion invariant or add explicit inheritance metadata; otherwise render that style’s missing content honestly.

14. **A client constant is not a real AI Tutor kill switch:** hiding launch sites leaves the Edge Function callable directly by authenticated clients, so the proposed “master” switch does not control service use or spend.
    Fix: Enforce disablement server-side as well, with the client flag used only for presentation.

15. **The census predicate can drift from runtime behavior:** the plan duplicates whitespace-trimming logic between a JavaScript script and TypeScript hook without shared fixtures or conformance tests.
    Fix: Test both implementations against the same empty, whitespace, null, and mixed-style fixtures—or generate both from one shared pure module.

16. **Failure observability is toast-only:** blocked Next/Previous/Jump/End operations will be indistinguishable in production, with no action, run-kind, retry, or deduplication context.
    Fix: Emit a structured, non-PII commit-failure event containing qid, run kind, requested transition, error category, and retry/dedupe state.

17. **The tests omit the declared refresh semantics:** `practiceByQid` intentionally disappears on refresh, but no remount/resume test proves the recorded attempt returns without persisting the practice choice.
    Fix: Add a remount test asserting the logged answer and answered oval return, the practice pick disappears, and no attempt/journal/Run Record mutation occurred.
